To whom it may concern

The Rendlesham Forest case is undoubtedly one of the best.documented and most
significant military encounters with a UFO. It is also very credible incident. The incident spanned
four days in 1980. Rendlesham Forest is a large pine forest, east of Ipswich, in Suffolk, England.
Nearby are the twin NATO air bases, RAF Bentwaters and RAF. Woodbridge; at the time both
bases were being leased to the United States Air Force, Several UFO incidents, including
multiple-witness sightings by military personnel, ground traces, and radioactive anomalies were
reported from Rendlesham forest. According to USAF security patrolmen on duty, "the object
was described as being metallic in apperance and triangular in shape, approx. 2-3 meters across
the base... it illuminated the entire forest with a white light, and had:a pulsing red light on top and
a bank(s) of blue lights underneath,” In November, 2002, the British Ministry of Defense released
the “Rendlesham File” of documents related to and confirming the Rendlesham Forest incident,

the “Rendiesham Forest Incident”. put the paragraph about the case so you know what f am
referring to so it would be easy for your agency to know what exactly what I am requesting, If
your agency does find any records pertaining to my request please send me the necessary fee
papers that I need if any records are to be located.

09-11-2009-150704-001

24 November 2009

Thank you for your email of 9 November about assessments made by the Ministry of Defence
in the last five years regarding: a) the likelihood of, or b) provisions for an alien invasion of the
United Kingdom. This has been considered as a request for information in accordance with
the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

MOD has not made any assessment of the likelinood of, or provisions for, an alien invasion of
the United Kingdom in the last five years.

It may be helpful if ! explain that the MOD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’
it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains
totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

request-222 10-d3ea8f6b @ whatdotheyknow.com Our Ref: 30-10-2009-094848-001

19 November 2009

Thank you for your e-mail to ClO-FO|@mod.uk regarding your request for all UFO sightings
over the last decade. This has been considered as a request for information in accordance with
the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MOD for the period 1998-2008 are available for viewing
on the MOD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. Reports for 2009 will be
published at the end of the year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended by future publication) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MOD is not obliged to comply with the part of your
request relating to records from 2009. Section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that the
MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the public as it
will enable them to have a more informed view of the MOD’s role in UFO matters, there is no
overriding reason why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be
argued, that by releasing files and the information they contain in chronological order, the public
will be able to follow the role of the MOD in a more logical and orderly fashion, and to view
actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is planned
and managed within the department responsible. The release of this information will undermine
this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other members of the public
may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release this
information at the moment.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal

C)

™
eed
INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

x

review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal
resolution has come to an end.

ww

If you remain dissatisfied foliowing an internal review, you may take your compiaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD
internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www.ico.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely

RAF Bus Sec 2

Thank you for your e-mail to CIO-FOI@mod.uk regarding your request for all UFO
sightings over the last decade. This has been considered as a request for
information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MOD for the 1998-2008 are available for
viewing on the MOD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”.
Reports for 2009 will be published at the end of the year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended by future
publication) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MOD is not obliged to
comply with this part of your request. Section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning
that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the
information.

While there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the
public as it will enable them to have a more informed view of the MOD’s role in UFO
matters, there is no overriding reason why the public should have the information
immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing files and the information
they contain in chronological order, the public will be able to follow the role of the
MOD in a more logical and orderly fashion, and to view actions in their correct
historical perspective.

1 Tf Yes, please attach details of PIT.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information
is planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this
information will undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files
(which other members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would
necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to
release this information at the moment.

I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

5 November 2009

Thank you for your e-mail to CIO-FO|@mod.uk regarding your request for all UFO sightings
over the last decade. This has been considered as a request for information in accordance with
the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MOD for the period 1998-2008 are available for viewing
on the MOD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. Reports for 2009 will be
published at the end of the year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended by future publication) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MOD is not obliged to comply with the part of your
request relating to records from 2009. Section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that the
MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the public as it
will enable them to have a more informed view of the MOD’s role in UFO matters, there is no
overriding reason why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be
k argued, that by releasing files and the information and+he-4mtermatien they contain in

chronological order, the public will be able to follow the role of the MOD in a more logical and
orderly fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is planned
and managed within the department responsible. The release of this information will undermine
this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other members of the public
may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release this
information at the moment.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal

Thank you for your e-mail to CIO-FO|@mod.uk regarding your request for all UFO sightings
over the last decade. This has been considered as a reques for information in accordance with

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MOD for thg,1998-2008 are available for viewing on the
MOD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. Reports for 2009 will be
published at the end of the year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended by future publication) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MOD is not obliged to comply with this part of your
request. Section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that the MOD must assess the balance
of public interest in release of the information.

While there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the public as it
will enable them to have a more informed view of the MOD’s role in UFO matters, there is no
overriding reason why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be
argued, that by releasing files and the information and the information they contain in
chronological order, the public will be able to follow the role of the MOD in a more logical and
orderly fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is planned
and managed within the department responsible. The release of this information will undermine
this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other members of the public
may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release this
information at the moment.

handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution

6 August 2009

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 10 July 2009 asking for details of UFO
sightings in Northern Ireland between 1959 and 2009.

With regard to sightings for the period 1959 to 1992, what records remain are available for
Public Viewing at The National Archives. You may wish to note that only a few files survived
before 1967 as MoD files were routinely destroyed after 5 years until then. The National
Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or
telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information
about the records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at
www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 are available for
viewing on the MoD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports“. | attach a copy
of the only sighting reported to us for Northern Ireland for 2009. You will note that names and
the address of the witness have been withheld under s.40 (Personal Information) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000.

UFO files covering the residual period of your request i.e.1993-96, are due for transfer to The
National Archives by the end of this year as part of a three year programme to release UFO
files from the Directorate of Air Staff and the Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period
from the late 1970’s to 2007.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future publication) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to comply with this part of your
request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that
the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the public as it
will enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in UFO matters, there is no

()

INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

.

overriding reason why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be
argued, that by releasing files and the information they contain in chronological order, the
oublic will be able to follow the role of the MoD in a more logical and orderly fashion, and to

- view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is
planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this information will
undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other members of
the public may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release this
information at this moment.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR @ mod.uk). Please note that any request
for an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt
to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of
the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http://www.ico.gov.uk.

The Constitution Unit, a research body at University College London, is carrying out an
independent evaluative study of the Freedom of Information Act 2000 in co-operation with a
number of public authorities, including the Ministry of Defence. An important part of the
evaluation process is gathering the experiences and opinions of FOI requesters like you. Any
information you provide will only be seen by the Constitution Unit and will be handled in
accordance with its privacy policy explained in the survey. The Ministry of Defence will only
see the published results: no individual responses will be visible to us and your participation
will have no effect on your future dealings with the Ministry.

If you would like to take part in this study, please visit the following link to be taken directly to
the survey: hitp://tinyurl.com/6prff8. Or, if you prefer, contact Ben Worthy at
b.worthy @ucl.ac.uk or or (0 ask more about the study.

If you have filled this survey out before, we invite you to fill it out again in light of the recent
response to your request.

Dear EE

Thank you for your UFO sighting report and your Freedom of Information request of 21 August
2009 asking for details of recent UFO reports. Additionally, you mentioned that you were interested
in sightings prior to 1967.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives, solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

I attach details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for January to August 2009.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 are available for viewing on

You might also be interested to know that the MOD has already released a great deal of information
about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after being
held for five years until 1967, when they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few
have survived before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public
viewing. They can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone,
020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the records they
hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

Additionally, we are part way through a three year programme to release UFO files from the

05/11/2009

Page 2 of 2

Airectorate of Air Staff and the Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period from the late 1970’s
‘© 2007. You may wish to consider contacting The National Archives press office for details of when
the next release is due.

T hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail

working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www.ico.gov.uk.

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

Details of UFO sightings for Northern Ireland for the period 1959-2009. Include the

Background

The files containing details of UFO files for the period 1959-92 are, if they exist,
already available at The National Archives.

Files for the remaining period (1993-1996) will be passed to The National Archives by
the end of this year.

Details of sightings for 1997-2008 are already available via the MoD website. We
have received only one sighting for Northern Ireland in 2009 which will be released
with name and the address redacted.

The following comments will be made to the applicant

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 10 July 2009 asking for
details of UFO sightings in Northern Ireland between 1959 and 2009.

With regard to sightings for the period 1959 to 1992, what records remain are
available for Public Viewing at The National Archives. You may wish to note that only
a few files survived before 1967 as MoD files were routinely destroyed after 5 years
until then. The National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond,
Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have

‘Tf Yes, please attach details of PIT.

a website giving information about the records they hold and how to access them.
This can be found on the internet at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 are available
for viewing on the MoD website www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. I
attach a copy of the only sighting reported to us for Northern Ireland for 2009. You
will note that names and the address of the witness have been withheld under s.40
(Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000.

UFO files covering the residual period of your request i.e.1993-96, are due for
transfer to The National Archives by the end of this year as part of a three year
programme to release UFO files from the Directorate of Air Staff and the Defence
Intelligence Staff covering the period from the late 1970's to 2007.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future
publication) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to
comply with this part of your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption,
meaning that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the
information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this information being released to the
public as it will enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in UFO
matters, there is no overriding reason why the public should have the information
immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing files and the information
they contain in chronological order, the public will be able to follow the role of the
MoD in a more logical and orderly fashion, and to view actions in their correct
historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information
is planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this
information will undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files
(which other members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would
necessarily have to be put off.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to
release this information at this moment.

I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

19-10-2009-163330-001

12 November 2009

Thank you for your e-mail of the 19 October 2009 requesting information about a possible
UFO sighting on 25 August 2009 in Dunblane, near Stirling at 2105hrs and if there had been
airborne activity in the area on that date and time. This has been considered as a request for
information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

| have examined our records and can find no report of a UFO sighting in the area on the date
and time specified. The MOD has no central record of aircraft flying in the area on the date
and time in question. In order to answer your question about airborne activity accurately, we
would need to check with every flying station in the UK whether they had any aircraft in the
area, the cost of which would exceed the compliance limit, set at £600, under Section 12 of
the Freedom of Information Act. This equates to one individual working for three and a half
days to locate, retrieve and extract the required information. Under the terms of Section 12 of
the FO! Act, we are therefore not obliged to comply with this element of your request.

It may be helpful if | explain that the MOD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’
it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

bd

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains
totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail CIO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for
an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to
reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of
the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http://www.ico.gov.uk.

| hope that this information is useful.

Yours sincerely

16-10-2009-111639-001

11 November 2009

Dear

Thank you for your e-mail of the 16 October 2009 requesting information about military aircraft
movements on 1 January 2009 at 0018GMT and whether military aircraft fly with headlights
on. This has been considered as a request for information in accordance with the Freedom of
Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

The MOD has no central record of aircraft flying in the area on the date and time in question.
In order to answer your question about airborne activity accurately, we would need to check
with every flying station in the UK whether they had any aircraft in the area, the cost of which
would exceed the compliance limit, set at £600, under Section 12 of the Freedom of
Information Act. This equates to one individual working for three and a half days to locate,
retrieve and extract the required information. Under the terms of Section 12 of the FOI Act,
we are therefore not obliged to comply with this element of your request.

Military aircraft have landing and navigation lights. Open source information on aircraft
lighting systems is available from a variety of outlets, such as the internet or reference books
in public libraries. Basic technical details of aircraft can be found in Jane’s ‘All the World’s
Aircraft’, which may be available from your local public library.

If your local library lacks the information, you may wish to make use of the internet. | did an
internet search which indicated that aircraft navigation lights are placed in a way similar to that
of marine vessels, with a red navigation light located on the left wingtip leading edge anda
green light on the right wingtip leading edge. A white navigation light is as far as possible to
the edge of the tail or each wing tip. High-intensity strobe lights are located on the aircraft to
aid in collision avoidance. Almost all modern aircraft are equipped with landing lights if they
are intended and approved for night-time operations. Landing lights are usually of very high
intensity because of the considerable distance that may separate an aircraft from terrain or

Vf
INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

£

© ROYAL HEADQUARTERS AIR COMMAND ©
1 I
obstacles; the landing lights of large aircraft can easily be seen by observers from several
miles away. @

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the |
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal |
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent

internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main

Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail CIO-FOI-IR @mod.uk). Please note that any request for

an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to

reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of
the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http:/Awww.ico.gov.uk.

| hope that this information is useful.

Our Ref:

oe e

Thank you for your emails of 5 October. They have been considered as a request for information in
accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOI). You asked for information regarding the
amount of serving members of the Armed Forces who have reported UFO’s or alien activity over the
last 12 months; where the incidents took place; what investigations took place and which were passed
onto other government agencies or related civilian agencies.

Firstly, it may be helpful if | explain that the MOD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it
receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance; namely,
whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the question
of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally open-minded.

| should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these
alleged phenomena. :

There is no requirement for individuals who correspond with the MOD, including those who report
sightings, to provide details of their employment. | can confirm, however, that in the last 12 months (1

a
LY

x
eed
INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

Y

{ r

October 2008 to 30 September 2009) from the records that MOD holds on UFO sightings reported to

the Department, there have been two sightings by people who identified themselves as serving e@
members of the armed forces: one sighting at Shepton Mallet reported by a Royal Navy individual and
one at Northolt reported by a Royal Air Force individual. Neither of these sightings prompted further
investigation or was passed to other Government or civilian agencies.

Yours sincerely

Please acknowledge within two working days that you are the appropriate area
to respond, or lead, and we will confirm this on AIT. You should respond to
the requester within twenty working days of the date the request was made. If
you consider another area is more appropriate to respond or lead, then please
approach them, also within two working days. Please engage with the FOI
Helpdesk at all times when you are looking to place a request elsewhere. The
FOI Helpdesk will be pleased to assist during this process.

This request will be automatically allocated to your area, on AIT, after three
working days, if the FOI Helpdesk receives no receipt or evidence of action to
place the request elsewhere.

If you are copied into this email, you may be a relevant contributor to the
response and you should liaise with the lead respondent.

This process is set out to maximise the amount of time to find information and
respond, when this request has been placed with the appropriate subject matter
expert.

about potential media interest in the FOI response please contact MOD Press

De

Thank you for your two Freedom of Information requests of 22 January 2009 asking about a
UFO sighting on the Isle of Lewis on January and near Bridgend on 3 January 2009. You wished to
know if there was any military activity in these areas at the time of the sightings and whether we had
received any similar reports. Please take this response to cover both requests.

The Ministry of Defence does not keep a central record of ali military aircraft activity and in order to
accurately state whether there was any military activity in the area, we would need to contact some
thirty flying stations across the UK, the cost which would exceed the £600 limit set for compliance
with the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of
Defence is not obliged to comply with your request.

Whilst we do have a centralised record of aircraft entering the UK Low Flying System, this system
was closed over the period in question and I would therefore very much doubt that the sightings were
caused by military activity.

No other similar sightings were made for the areas in question over this period.

I am sorry I can not be more helpful.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the

Director of Information Exploitation, 6h Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-
mail InfoXD @mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

http://www. informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

27 August 2009

Thank you for your Freedom of Information (FOI) request of 28 Jui 2009 asking
whether there had been any military activity over Edinburgh at about 00:36 hours on Sunday
20" July 2009 and whether any reports that lights had been seen on that date over Edinburgh
had been received.

The MoD has no central record of aircraft flying over Edinburgh on the date and time in
question. In order for us to accurately answer your question, we would need to check with
every flying station in the UK whether they had any aircraft in the area, the cost of which
would exceed the £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act. This
represents the cost of one person spending three and a half working days in determining
whether the Department holds the information, and locating, retrieving and extracting the
information. Under the terms of Section 12 of the FOI Act, this means that we are not obliged
to comply with this element of your request at this time.

However, | can confirm that we have received no other reports of UFO sightings over
Edinburgh for 19 or 20" July 2009.

It is of course possible that military aircraft may have been transiting the area, and that
Edinburgh’s civilian Air Traffic Control may have some record.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR @ mod.uk). Please note that any request
for an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt
to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the

From: Air CmdSecCS-RAFBusSec10 Ramee

To: Air CmdSecCS-RAFParliBusiness (MULTIUSER)
Subject: 20090713 FOI 09-07-2009-144456-053 UFO Sightings1998/1999-u

Thank you for your e-mail of 7 July 2009 asking for all Northern Ireland UFO sightings in
1998/1999.

Firstly, it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any
reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen
might have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the
United Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air
activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external
source, and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to
identify the precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if
resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MoD to provide this
kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if
we were to do so.

27/10/2009

Page 2 of 3

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or
@ question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it
remains totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence
which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

Details of UFO sightings for the period 1997-2008 can be found on the MoD website
www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. For the current year until the 30 June, the
MoD has recorded 240 reports relating to 2009, a further 8 relating to 2008 which were not
received in time to appear on the website and 1 for 2007. Details of sightings for 2009 will
be placed on the MoD website in early 2010.

| hope this is helpful.

to the right at tremendous speed before returning. It then drifted slowly away. It was louder than a plane or helicopter.

A bright red light over the house.

Single small white orb shortly joined by two others. Changed into a dull flat circular shape. They were hovering over Central
London at 15-20,000 ft but not blown by the wind. Objects disappeared but one reappeared for 5 minutes

Six very bright orange lights flying in formation passed by in 15 minutes. Heading from Upper Norwood towards Crystal
Palace

Twenty orange balls heading north.

Thank you for your e-mail of the 27 October 2009 requesting information about the number of
UFO’s that have been recovered from UK soil in the last 10 years. This has been considered
as a request for information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Our Ref: 28-10-2009-11 1613-01

28 October 2009

There has been no recovery of any UFO from UK soil.

It may be helpful if | explain that the MOD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’
it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do §0.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains
totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main

Thank you for your email of 15 September. This has been considered as a request for
information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

You asked:

Was there any military activity taking place in this area at this time?
If so, what type of aircraft was involved?

What was the purpose?

Have there been any reports of UFOs from this area and time?

Is the MOD aware of any explanation for this sighting?

There was no military activity taking place in the Wirral area around 11.00pm on 5 September
2009. Consequently there were no military aircraft involved and as such the question of purpose
does not arise. However, our enquiries have confirmed that there were three civilian aircraft
around the area at the time stated by you.

| have not been able to trace any reports, so far, of UFOS for the area you asked for at the time
requested.

The MOD is unaware of any explanation for this sighting. It may be helpful if | explain that the
MOD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether
what was seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence
that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air
activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the

precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this
purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains

totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

Subject: Release-authorised: FO! Request 08-09-2009-171435-001 fiaiamianhU4.0|

Dear RAC,

We have received a FO! request regarding a UFO sighting that was reported in the Warminster area
on the night of Saturday 29 Aug 09 at 22:33. The requestor is asking if there were “any UAV’s or
other military aircraft were flying on this flight path at that time’. The sighting details are as
follows: “The object was sighted from an east facing view point on Cradle Hill near Elm Hill,
Warminister and was travelling in a north-north-east direction before changing to an easterly
direction and disappearing into the distance. “

Thank you for your email requesting information a UFO sighting in the Warminster area on
Saturday, 29 August 2009 at 22:33. | am replying as this is an area that falls within Air
Command's remit. This has been considered as a request for information in accordance with
the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Our Ref: 08-09-2009-171435-001

30 September 2009

You asked us to confirm whether any UAVs or military aircraft were flying at this time. | can
confirm that no UAVs or military aircraft were in the area at the time in question. However, a
Police helicopter was operating in the vicinity. For your information | have also checked our
records and there are no other reported sightings for the Warminster area for that date.

| hope you find this information useful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If-informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail CIO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an
internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to reach
informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http:/Avww.ico.gov.uk.

Radar Heads Clee Hill, London Heathrow and Pease Pottage
Recording starts 292115ZAug 09 Recording ends 292145Z Aug 09

The Reference Point (RP) used is N51 13 W002 09, which is the white cross located in the centre of the
screen.
Synopsis of Events Based on Radar Replay: Clee Hill London Heathrow radars.

I looked at each of the above stated radars from and although no aircraft were seen to pass directly over the
RP the following was observed:

London Heathrow Radar
At 21.16:56 an intermittent primary only radar return is observed (this is the + symbol) 6.0nm NW of the RP,
this continued slowly on a SE heading until at 21.33:53 it passes 2.0nm NE of the RP still on the SE heading.

Clee Hill Radar
This is the only identifiable aircraft that passes close to the RP:
At 21.41 S0032/013 passes 2.2nm W of the RP heading from N to S.

Comments by RACO:

CD contents as follows:
One 15nm range recording produced at 4 times normal speed (London Heathrow).
One 15nm range recording produced at 4 times normal speed. (Clee Hill).

This is not a particularly good area for low level coverage. The slow moving primary track heads directly
towards Boscombe Down which it passes at 2159:54. It maintains a SE heading passing 5.7nm N of
Southampton airport at 2230:59. The return finally fades from radar in the vicinity of Midhurst at 22:56:09.
It has not been possible to identify the slow moving primary only track.

Thank you for your e-mail of 31 August 2009 concerning the strange occurrence you witnessed
over the Doncaster area on Saturday, 30 August 2009. | have assumed that you are referring to
Sunday 30 August, but for completeness | have checked both dates. | am replying as this is an
area that falls within Air Command’s remit. This has been considered as a request for
information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

Our Ref: 02-09-2009-131341-002

28 September 2009

You asked us to check with RAF Waddington for any military flights operating in the Doncaster
area on that date. | have confirmed that there were no military flights over the Doncaster area
for either the 29 or 30 August 2009. A radar trace was also conducted for both those dates but
nothing was found to match the events you reported. For your information no other UFO reports
have been received for the Doncaster area for those dates.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this
purpose, but it is not the function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. | should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

| am sorry | could not be more helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. if informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail CIO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an
internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to reach
informal resolution has come to an end.

drinking. My view was outof the window over the rooftops towards Doncaster Royal infirmary and the town
centre.

At 856pm, a bright orange light emerged from the distance coming towards our house.at first | thought it
was the Police helicopter SY99 which flies around this area quite regularly-it wasnt. there was no noise of the
engines or of the chopping sound as it approached.the orange ball,had no navigation lights to front or the
side/wing tips..lt was about the same size as an helicopter,shape not discernable. It veered to its left and
accelerated going north towards Barnby dun. Its height was about normal helicopter height,maybe a little
higher- the speed was faster than the police sy99.

This object was out of sight within 5-6 seconds.

Within 3 mins, 2 more separate orange objects appeared,same route,same trajectory-they were spaced
about 300 metres apart-they went on same route north. | went outside on my front driveway to have a look-
no reflections,the 2 objects accelerated out of view ascending slightly.

My wife cameout with me to have a look,she likewise was looking in awe.

In another 2 mins another 2 orange objects appeared,took same route again and vanished, | rushed
upstairs to get my Nikon video camera,to get a quick shot of them; by the time | got downstairs they had
gone. As | set my camera up,another single orange object(the 6th) appeared the same way and travelled
North as the previous 5.._ The time now was 9.06pm. | was not able to take a photo. | have since left my
camera next to me in case it happens again.
| have no idea what the 6 objects were,bright orange shapes,under own propulsion-no navigation lights.

Thank you for your e-mail of 18 August 2009, concerning your report of strange flying objects
seen during the night of Saturday 8 August 2009. | am replying as this is an area that falls
within Air Command’s remit. This has been considered as a request for information in
accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FOIA).

You asked if there were any MOD operations that evening. | have been unable to trace records
for any military operations in the area of Twyford for that date. | have also check our records
and no other unusual sightings have been reported. Also you asked the question if the MOD
have any airplanes which are static. The only aircraft we have that are capable of remaining
static in mid-air are helicopters and Harrier jets.

\f you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an
internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to reach
informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www.ico.gov.uk.
| hope that this information is useful.

Yours sincerely

[Original Signed]

RAF Business Secretariat 10

Our Ref: TOC2009

22 September 2009

Thank you for your email of 14 September about lights on aircraft.

It may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence (MOD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives, solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

However, open source information on aircraft lighting systems is available from a variety of
outlets, such as the internet or from reference books in public libraries. Basic technical details
of aircraft can be found in Jane's All the World's Aircraft, which may be available from your
local public library.

If your local library lacks the information you seek, you may wish to make use of the internet. |
did an internet search which indicated that aircraft navigation lights are placed in a way similar
to that of marine vessels, with a red navigation light located on the left wingtip leading edge
and a green light on the right wingtip leading edge. A white navigation light is as far as
possible to the edge of the tail or each wing tip. High-intensity strobe lights are located on the
aircraft to aid in collision avoidance.

Navigation lights in civil aviation are required to be operational and to be turned on from
sunset to sunrise. High-intensity white strobe lights are part of the anti-collision light system,
as well as the aviation red or white rotating beacon. The anti-collision light system (either
strobe lights or rotating beacon) is required to be operational and to be turned on for all
airplanes built after 11 March 1996 on all aircraft operations.

()

INVESTORS IN PEOPLE

e.. further information on lighting specifications you may also wish to contact the Civil
Aviation Authority. Their Library and Information Centre, located at Aviation House, Gatwick
Airport South, West Sussex RH6 OYR, contains a collection of books, reports, directories,
statistics, videos, and periodicals on aviation. The CAA Library is open to members of the
public Monday to Friday from 09.30 to 16.30 (10.00 to 16.30 on the first Wednesday of the

month) who are welcome to use the Library for reference purposes. A photocopier and
microfilm/microfiche reader printers are available for public use. Charges are payable for
copies and microform printouts. The Library can be contacted on 01293 573725, Fax 01293
573181, or e-mail infoservices @caa.co.uk

You also asked for what happens to sighting reports received by the MOD. Reports of
sightings are recorded and acknowledged whenever contact details are provided. Although
the overwhelming majority of the UFO desk’s work involves (and has for decades) answering
correspondence from the public or recording sightings reported to the MoD, we do on
occasion investigate reports. This usually involves liaising with air defence specialists (e.g.
radar specialists) on perhaps half a dozen reports per year. These will generally be sightings
made by aircrew, members of the armed forces or the police.

UFO files are released annually to the National Archives — please follow this link for more
details: http://ufos.nationalarchives.gov.uk/

| hope this is helpful to you.
Yours sincerely

RAF Business Secretariat 9

2.

a *

Thanks for that. However, | have found this generic paragraph on Wikipedia. Please can you
confirm that military aircraft generically conform with this lighting pattern.

Aircraft navigation lights are placed in a way similar to that of marine vessels, with a red navigation
light located on the left wingtip leading edge and a green light on the right wingtip leading edge. A
white navigation light is as far aft as possible on the tail or each wing tip. High-intensity strobe lights
are located on the aircraft to aid in collision avoidance.

Navigation lights in civil aviation are required to be operational and to be turned on from sunset to
sunrise. High-intensity white strobe lights are part of the anti-collision light system, as well as the
aviation red or white rotating beacon. The anti collision light system (either strobe lights or rotating

beacon) is required to be operational and to be turned on for all airplanes built after March 11, 1996
on all aircraft operations.

Many thanks for your help.

Regards

RAF Bus Sec 10

[oS

Subject: Release-Authorised: 20090921-FOI Response REMI 0 Sightings-21-08-2009-131118-001-U

Thank you for your UFO sighting report and your Freedom of Information request of 21 August
2009 asking for details of recent UFO reports. Additionally, you mentioned that you were interested
in sightings prior to 1967.

Firstly. it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives, solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance: namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of “UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

T attach details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for January to August 2009.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 are available for viewing on
the MoD website ww .nod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”.

You might also be interested to know that the MOD has already released a great deal of information
about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after being
held for five years until 1967, when they were generaily preserved for The National Archives. A few
have survived before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public
viewing. They can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone.
020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the records they
hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk,

Additionally, we are part way through a three year programme to release UFO files from the

21/09/2009
Page 2 of 2

Directorate of Air Staff and the Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period from the late 1970’
to 2007. You may wish to consider contacting The National Archives press office for details of when
the next release is due.

{ hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
C1lO-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40

On Mon, Aug 10, 2009 at 12:05 AM, Air CmdSecCS-RAFBusScc! 3 aaa
<AirCmdSecCS-RAFBusSec 13 @mod.uk> wrote:

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 30 July 2009.

Your request was quite brief, but I assume from that you would like to see any documentation the
Ministry of Defence (MoD) holds on the Rendlesham case between the dates 12 November 1980
and | January 2009.

The Rendlesham case is very popular amongst those members of the public interested in UFO
matters and the MoD receives a considerable amount of correspondence on the matter. The
overwhelming bulk of the information the MoD holds on the Rendlesham case is simply letters
and e-mails such as yours asking for information.

141408-004-U

Dear Sir or Madam

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 18 August 2009 asking for a copy of the
MoD file on the Rendlesham Incident. Additionally, you asked why it had been withdrawn [rom the
MoD website but could be available in response to an FOI request and why the proactive release date
shown on the website was earlier than the date of the actual incident.

The MoD file on the Rendlesham Incident is available to view at The National Archives website at
www. nationalarchives.gov.uk

The reason that the Rendlesham file had been withdrawn from the MoD website for copyright
reasons but was available under the Freedom of Information Act, is because some 40% of the
information on the file is correspondence from the public. This material is subject to the Copyright
Designs and Patents Act 1988, which prohibits its publication on the internet. When this was
realised, it was decided to remove the file from the MoD website. However. the MoD has an
overriding requirement to release material under FOL, which does not count as “publication” by the
Department.

Finally the dates shown on the website are incorrect due to an administrative error.
T hope this is helpful.

If you are not satistied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request. then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
CIO-FOI-IR @ mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www. ico.gov.uk.

Thank you for your e-mail of 28 August 2009 and the photographs taken in Blackpool in October
2008. You asked whether there were any manoeuvres in the area to explain the craft pictured in
the photographs. | am replying as this is an area that falls within Air Command’s remit. This has
been considered as a request for information in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act
2000 (FOIA).

Our Ref: 02-09-2009-122742-020

16 September 2009

| can confirm that the MOD does have records on military manoeuvres, but there were no Royal
Air Force or Army manoeuvres in the Blackpool area in October 2008.

| also asked the Royal Navy (RN) to check their records. To provide the information you
requested we would have to examine the log of every UK-based RN ship to determine whether
it (or it’s aircraft, if appropriate) was in the vicinity during the specified period. The cost of
obtaining this information would exceed the £600 limit set for compliance with the FOIA. This
represents the cost of one person spending three and a half working days in determining
whether the Department hold the information, and locating, retrieving and extracting the
information. Under the terms of Section 12 of the FOIA this means that we are not obliged to
comply with this element of your request.

| am sorry | could not be more helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution
is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal
review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building,
Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail CIO-FOI-IR@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an
internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt to reach
informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http:/Avww.ico.gov.uk.

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 14 August 2009 asking for information
for UFO sightings for 2009.

| attach details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for January to August 2009.
Additionally, details of UFO sightings for the period 1997-2008 can be found on the MoD

You might also be interested to know that the MOD has already released a great deal of
information about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely
destroyed after being held for five years until 1967, when they were generally preserved for
The National Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these together with records up
to 1992 are now available for public viewing. They can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue,
Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also
have a website giving information about the records they hold and how to access them. This
can be found on the internet at www.nationalarchives gov uk.

Additionally, we are part way through a three year programme to release UFO files from the
Directorate of Air Staff and the Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period from the late
1970's to 2007. You may wish to consider contacting The National Archives press office for
details of when the next release is due.

| hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Fioor, MOD Main

15 September 2009

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request regarding UFOs. You asked
how far back the Ministry of Defence has known about UFOs and for a list of UFOs reported
in the UK and for any true videos or film of UFOs.

Firstly, it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports
of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. lt would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/lying saucer matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains
totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena and therefore are not aware of any
film or video of “true” UFOs.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 can be found on our
website at www.mod.uk by searching under UFO Reports. | appreciate that you do not have
the internet, but your local public library should have internet capability and they should be
able to assist you. | attach details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period
January-August 2009. :

You may also wish to know that since 1967, following an increase in public interest in this
subject, "UFO" report files are now routinely preserved. Files for 1967 to 1995, and any files
prior to 1967 which did survive, are now available for examination at The National Archives,

Dear Sir

circling the area several times before I went out with my camera which was a
cold cloudy day, when I started to video the jet at the same time this UFO just
popped out from behind a cloud going all over the place & back into the
clouds then there were others doing the same but only two of them just kept
popping in & out of the clouds while the rest just stayed behind thin layers of
the clouds I could just make out.

All the others were taken by different cameras I have & one that struck me
the most of all when I did not have my camera then but I watched it for about
20 minutes it was all black, no port windows, no strobe lights, no sign of
where the engines were as one would on any air craft, no sound at all very
quiet, but underneath was what struck me most of all?

This UFO, would be about 40/60 feet circular the height from the bottom of
the UFO to the top part about 15/20 if no more? Underneath this craft had
two different shaped type boxes then all the area of it had earth military type
camouflage we use on all vehicles & planes, it was about 500/1000feet above
me & it just look likes a giant Frisbee! This was August 1996 Sunday A very
clear blue sky it was 1:15pm, once I got home I made a model of it and made
a rough birds eye view of it’s path as it went over the Solway cost over into
England towards the East coast?. I keep a kind of record of them so that I can
keep track & numbers I’ve seen & caught on my cameras over the years.

Further to my letter of 21 August | am now in a position to confirm that a check of our records
for the area adjacent to Alton Barnes around 1400 on 26 June this year, shows that a few
aircraft were operating in that area at that time. Unfortunately, these records do not
distinguish whether the aircraft were military or civilian. Whilst two aircraft in. particular could
be observed, it is not possible to identify these aircraft, however it is possible that one was a
military Gazelle aircraft operating out of Middle Wallop.

| hope this is helpful and please accept my apologies for the earlier confusion.

27 August 2009

Thank you for your Freedom of Information (FOI) request of 28 Jul 2009 asking
whether there had been any military activity over Edinburgh at about 00:36 hours on Sunday
20" July 2009 and whether any reports that lights had been seen-on that date over Edinburgh
had been received.

The MoD has no central record of aircraft flying over Edinburgh on the date and time in
question. In order for us to accurately answer your question, we would need to check with
every flying station in the UK whether they had any aircraft in the area, the cost of which
would exceed the £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act. This
represents the cost of one person spending three and a half working days in determining
whether the Department holds the information, and locating, retrieving and extracting the
information. Under the terms of Section 12 of the FOI Act, this means that we are not obliged
to comply with this element of your request at this time.

However, | can confirm that we have received no other reports of UFO sightings over
Edinburgh for 19 or 20" July 2009.

It is of course possible that military aircraft may have been transiting the area, and that
Edinburgh’s civilian Air Traffic Control may have some record.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
intemal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail ClO-FOI-IR @mod.uk). Please note that any request
for an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date on which the attempt
to.reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
